# Stage 2 fine-tune PoC — result

Date: 2026-07-06. Status: **complete. Verdict: NOT-JUSTIFIED** (non-expert drafter
fine-tuning does not materially raise p=1 acceptance). Implements
`summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md` (Activities 1–9). Self-consistent torch MPS
drafter (float32; body structurally faithful, head bit-faithful).

## Verdict

**NOT-JUSTIFIED.** Head-only LoRA (the lever identified by Activity 4) raises held-out
p=1 by **+0.60 pp** (0.8118 → 0.8179; McNemar p=0.012, net +43/7137 anchors) — a
statistically-real but **practically-tiny** effect, far below the +5 pp reaffirm gate
and below +1 pp after the required rank 32/64/128 sweep. Body/input-side LoRA
(Activity 8) was not run: its trigger condition (Activity 7 < Activity 6 ceiling) is
unmet (0.8186 > 0.7479), and Activity 4 independently shows the drafter is
**input-invariant at p=1** (input_effect 0.0, input_shift_rate 1.85%), so input-side
adaptation is expected to be negligible. The decision: **close the non-expert
fine-tuning direction.**

## Evidence (Activities 4/6/7/9)

- **Activity 4 (crossed hidden/label oracle):** on 54 prefix-aligned cells, the
  drafter is **input-invariant** at p=1 (input_effect 0.0; the p=1 prediction is
  identical on IQ2XXS vs Q4-tap hidden states 98% of the time); **label/argmax drift
  dominates** (label_effect 1.85 pp, label_shift_rate 9.26%). ⇒ the lever is the
  output/head ordering, NOT the input representation.
- **Activity 6 (from-scratch head ceiling [GATE]):** no-LoRA frozen baseline p=1 =
  **0.8118** (matches the ~0.81 reference; confirms the self-consistent torch
  baseline). From-scratch head trained on the frozen pre-head features reaches only
  **0.7479** held-out (below the pretrained head) — from-scratch under-generalizes
  (21k Q2 anchors vs the pretrained head's FP-teacher training), as the plan-review
  codex predicted. **Overfit-to-eval 0.9947** ⇒ the head impl is correct and `h`
  carries the target info (target recoverable). Gate decision: proceed to 7 (not an
  info deficit).
- **Activity 7 (head-only LoRA, rank sweep 32/64/128):** **+0.67 / +0.62 / +0.63 pp** —
  **flat across rank** (more capacity does not help → information-limited, not
  capacity-limited). Overfit-to-eval (rank 32) only 0.8223 — rank-32 LoRA cannot even
  fit the eval set. Best +0.67 pp ⇒ **NOT-JUSTIFIED** for the head path.
- **Activity 9 (final eval + McNemar):** head-LoRA rank 32 **+0.60 pp** (0.8118 →
  0.8179); McNemar discordants pre-only 119 / post-only 162 (p=0.012) — a real but tiny
  net effect. Activity 8 skipped (7 > 6 ceiling; A4 input-negligible).

## Interpretation

- The Q2-target drafter deficit is **shallow** (Stage 0: target usually rank-2 in the
  drafter's distribution) and **label/ordering-side** (Activity 4), but it is **not
  recoverable by non-expert head LoRA**: the head LoRA is information-limited (rank
  sweep flat; can't fit eval) and yields only ~+0.6 pp.
- Consistent with Stage 1 (raising tap-layer precision did not help acceptance) and
  Stage 0's caveat that the shallow-error *shape* is not proof of *recoverability*.
- The from-scratch ceiling (0.7479 < pretrained 0.8118) signals the Q2 features
  generalize worse than the FP features the pretrained head learned on — i.e. the Q2
  mismatch does degrade the feature information, but not in a way a non-expert head
  LoRA can exploit for a material gain.

## Methodology notes (self-consistency + chaos)

- The torch MPS drafter (body+head, float32) is used **self-consistently** for features,
  baseline, and eval; the decision rule measures *relative* improvement, valid regardless
  of the inherent float32 BLAS chaos offset vs the numpy oracle.
- **Head bit-faithful** to numpy `forward_head` (argmax 100%, top-128 100%, base
  max-abs 3e-5). **Body structurally faithful** (layer-0 parity max-abs ~2e-4 after the
  codex-found Sinkhorn first-row eps-placement fix); deeper-layer float32 BLAS chaos is
  inherent (a numpy replay reproduces it from BLAS differences alone) and accepted.
- Codex review of this verdict + methodology: retained at
  `artifacts/stage2_plan_review/` (this run) and `/tmp/` (body-bug review).

## Recommendation

- **Close the non-expert fine-tuning direction** (head LoRA: +0.6 pp; body LoRA not
  run, gated out by Activity 4 + the 7>6 condition).
- **Expert tuning** is the remaining untested lever but is **out of scope** (separate
  workstream). If pursued later, the from-scratch ceiling + crossed-oracle findings
  suggest it would need to address the Q2 feature degradation upstream of the head.
- Otherwise, redirect to **server-side multi-request batching** (amortize the verify
  bandwidth floor) — the local single-request speculative path on this target/drafter
  pair has now exhausted drafter-precision (Stage 0 quantization ceiling), tap-precision
  (Stage 1), tree structure (`spec_speedup_model.md` Q3), and non-expert fine-tuning
  (this PoC) without clearing baseline.

## Artifacts

- Features + results: `dspark_train/data/{train,eval}_torch_features.safetensors`,
  `activity{6,7,9}*.json`.
- Harnesses: `dspark_train/{drafter_body,drafter_head,run_stage2_*}.py`.
- Captured data: `dspark_train/data/shards/` (Activity 3); corpus `prompts/stage2_corpus/`.
- Engine change: `--capture-dataset` mode + multi-layer patch (ds4 repo, this branch).

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 7 — head-only LoRA rank sweep (32/64/128) on the pretrained head.
     3	
     4	Builds the pretrained DrafterHead with LoRA adapters (zero-init -> starts == frozen),
     5	trains the LoRA params on p=1 CE over train features, sweeps rank 32/64/128, reports
     6	held-out p=1 vs the no-LoRA baseline. Decision rule: reaffirm >=+5pp; not-justified
     7	<+1pp after the rank sweep. Activity 8 trigger: best LoRA < Activity 6 ceiling.
     8	"""
     9	from __future__ import annotations
    10	
    11	import json
    12	import sys
    13	from pathlib import Path
    14	
    15	import numpy as np
    16	import torch
    17	
    18	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    19	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_train"))
    20	from drafter_head import build_head   # noqa
    21	GGUF = HERE.parent.parent / "ds4" / "gguf"
    22	DSPARK = str((GGUF / "dspark.gguf").resolve())
    23	TARGET = str((GGUF / "DeepSeek-V4-Flash-IQ2XXS-w2Q2K-AProjQ8-SExpQ8-OutQ8-chat-v2-imatrix.gguf").resolve())
    24	DATA = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data"
    25	DEV = "mps"
    26	
    27	
    28	def load_features(split):
    29	    from safetensors import safe_open
    30	    f = DATA / f"{split}_torch_features.safetensors"
    31	    with safe_open(f, "numpy") as d:
    32	        return (torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("x").astype(np.float32)),
    33	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("anchor").astype(np.int64)),
    34	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("target_p1").astype(np.int64)))
    35	
    36	
    37	def p1(head, x, anchor, target, bs=256):
    38	    n = x.shape[0]; m = 0; head.eval()
    39	    with torch.no_grad():
    40	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    41	            s = head.p1_scores(x[i:i+bs, 0], anchor[i:i+bs])
    42	            m += (s.argmax(-1).cpu() == target[i:i+bs]).sum().item()
    43	    return m / n
    44	
    45	
    46	def train(head, x, a, t, epochs=12, lr=3e-4, bs=256, wd=0.01, tag=""):
    47	    params = [p for p in head.parameters() if p.requires_grad]
    48	    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(params, lr=lr, weight_decay=wd)
    49	    n = x.shape[0]; head.train()
    50	    for ep in range(epochs):
    51	        perm = torch.randperm(n); tl = 0.0
    52	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    53	            idx = perm[i:i+bs]
    54	            s = head.p1_scores(x[idx, 0], a[idx])
    55	            loss = torch.nn.functional.cross_entropy(s, t[idx].to(DEV))
    56	            opt.zero_grad(); loss.backward(); opt.step(); tl += loss.item() * len(idx)
    57	        if (ep + 1) % 6 == 0 or ep == 0:
    58	            print(f"  {tag}epoch {ep+1}: CE {tl/n:.4f}", flush=True)
    59	
    60	
    61	def main():
    62	    xtr, atr, ttr = load_features("train"); xev, aev, tev = load_features("eval")
    63	    print(f"train {xtr.shape[0]} eval {xev.shape[0]}", flush=True)
    64	    a6 = json.loads((DATA / "activity6_summary.json").read_text())
    65	    results = {}
    66	    for rank in [32, 64, 128]:
    67	        head = build_head(DSPARK, TARGET, device=DEV, lora_rank=rank, dtype=torch.float32)
    68	        base = p1(head, xev, aev, tev)
    69	        train(head, xtr, atr, ttr, epochs=12, lr=3e-4, tag=f"r{rank} ")
    70	        lp = p1(head, xev, aev, tev)
    71	        print(f"rank {rank}: no-LoRA {base:.4f} -> LoRA {lp:.4f} (delta {(lp-base)*100:+.2f} pp)", flush=True)
    72	        results[rank] = {"baseline": base, "lora": lp, "delta_pp": (lp - base) * 100}
    73	    best_rank = max(results, key=lambda r: results[r]["lora"])
    74	    best = results[best_rank]["lora"]; base0 = results[32]["baseline"]
    75	    print("\n=== Activity 7 RANK SWEEP ===")
    76	    print(f"  baseline (no-LoRA): {base0:.4f}")
    77	    for r in [32, 64, 128]:
    78	        print(f"  rank {r}: {results[r]['lora']:.4f} ({results[r]['delta_pp']:+.2f} pp)")
    79	    print(f"  best: rank {best_rank} = {best:.4f} ({(best-base0)*100:+.2f} pp)")
    80	    print(f"  from-scratch ceiling (A6): {a6['head_only_ceiling_p1']:.4f}")
    81	    gate = (best - base0) * 100
    82	    verdict = ("head path REAFFIRM (>=+5pp)" if gate >= 5
    83	               else f"head path NOT-JUSTIFIED (<+1pp after rank 32/64/128 sweep); best +{gate:.2f}pp" if gate < 1
    84	               else f"head path INCONCLUSIVE ({gate:+.2f}pp)")
    85	    print(f"  verdict: {verdict}")
    86	    print(f"  Activity 8 trigger (best<A6ceiling?): {'YES' if best < a6['head_only_ceiling_p1'] else 'NO'}")
    87	    (DATA / "activity7_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps({
    88	        "baseline_p1": base0, "rank_sweep": {str(r): results[r] for r in [32, 64, 128]},
    89	        "best_rank": best_rank, "best_p1": best, "best_delta_pp": (best - base0) * 100,
    90	        "from_scratch_ceiling_p1": a6["head_only_ceiling_p1"], "verdict": verdict}, indent=2) + "\n")
    91	
    92	
    93	if __name__ == "__main__":
    94	    main()

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 5/6/7 — DSpark drafter HEAD in torch (MPS), RESEARCH INSTRUMENTATION.
     3	
     4	Faithful port of dspark_oracle/forward.py forward_head + hc_primitives.hc_head/rmsnorm:
     5	  x [b, BLOCK, HC, DIM]  (frozen pre-head feature, from the numpy oracle body)
     6	  -> hc_head -> rmsnorm -> base_logits = x @ lm_head.T
     7	  -> autoregressive markov rollout -> output_ids [b, BLOCK+1]
     8	
     9	Weights loaded once via dspark_oracle/gguf_loader. LoRA adapters (hc_head_fn, norm,
    10	markov_w1, markov_w2) are zero-init -> at construction the head is bit-identical to the
    11	numpy forward_head (Activity 5 fidelity gate); Activity 6/7 train the LoRA params.
    12	"""
    13	from __future__ import annotations
    14	
    15	import sys
    16	from pathlib import Path
    17	
    18	import numpy as np
    19	import torch
    20	import torch.nn as nn
    21	
    22	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent            # issue468/
    23	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_oracle"))
    24	from gguf_loader import load_gguf_dense_only, index_gguf, read_tensor  # noqa: E402
    25	
    26	HC, DIM, BLOCK, VOCAB = 4, 4096, 5, 129280
    27	NORM_EPS, HC_EPS = 1e-6, 1e-6
    28	
    29	
    30	def _t(a):
    31	    return torch.as_tensor(np.ascontiguousarray(a))
    32	
    33	
    34	class DrafterHead(nn.Module):
    35	    def __init__(self, hc_fn, hc_scale, hc_base, norm_w, markov_w1, markov_w2, lm_head,
    36	                 lora_rank: int = 0):
    37	        super().__init__()
    38	        self.register_buffer("hc_fn", _t(hc_fn))              # [HC, HC*DIM]
    39	        self.register_buffer("hc_scale", _t(hc_scale))        # [1]
    40	        self.register_buffer("hc_base", _t(hc_base))          # [HC]
    41	        self.register_buffer("norm_w", _t(norm_w))            # [DIM]
    42	        self.register_buffer("markov_w1", _t(markov_w1))      # [VOCAB, rank_m]
    43	        self.register_buffer("markov_w2", _t(markov_w2))      # [VOCAB, rank_m]
    44	        self.register_buffer("lm_head", _t(lm_head))          # [VOCAB, DIM]
    45	        self.lora_rank = lora_rank
    46	        self.rank_m = markov_w1.shape[1]
    47	        r = lora_rank
    48	        if r > 0:
    49	            self.lora_hc_A = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(r, HC * DIM))
    50	            self.lora_hc_B = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(HC, r))
    51	            self.lora_norm = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(DIM))
    52	            self.lora_mw1_A = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(r, self.rank_m))
    53	            self.lora_mw1_B = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(VOCAB, r))
    54	            self.lora_mw2_A = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(r, self.rank_m))
    55	            self.lora_mw2_B = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(VOCAB, r))
    56	
    57	    def _hc_fn(self):
    58	        return self.hc_fn + (self.lora_hc_B @ self.lora_hc_A) if self.lora_rank else self.hc_fn
    59	
    60	    def _norm_w(self):
    61	        return self.norm_w * (1.0 + self.lora_norm) if self.lora_rank else self.norm_w
    62	
    63	    def _mw1(self, ids):
    64	        w1 = self.markov_w1 + (self.lora_mw1_B @ self.lora_mw1_A) if self.lora_rank else self.markov_w1
    65	        return w1[ids]
    66	
    67	    def _mw2_T(self, dtype):
    68	        w2 = self.markov_w2 + (self.lora_mw2_B @ self.lora_mw2_A) if self.lora_rank else self.markov_w2
    69	        return w2.T.to(dtype)
    70	
    71	    def hc_head(self, x):  # x [b, BLOCK, HC, DIM] -> [b, BLOCK, DIM]
    72	        b, s, hc, d = x.shape
    73	        flat = x.reshape(b, s, hc * d)
    74	        rsqrt = 1.0 / torch.sqrt((flat * flat).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)
    75	        mixes = (flat @ self._hc_fn().T) * rsqrt                 # [b, BLOCK, HC]
    76	        pre = torch.sigmoid(mixes * self.hc_scale[0] + self.hc_base) + HC_EPS
    77	        return (pre.unsqueeze(-1) * x).sum(dim=2)                # [b, BLOCK, DIM]
    78	
    79	    def p1_scores(self, x0, anchor):  # x0 [N,HC,DIM], anchor [N] -> [N,VOCAB] (LoRA-applied)
    80	        dev = self.lm_head.device; dt = self.lm_head.dtype
    81	        x = x0.to(dev).unsqueeze(1)                       # [N,1,HC,DIM]
    82	        h = self.hc_head(x)[:, 0].to(dev, dt)             # [N,DIM] (uses _hc_fn with LoRA)
    83	        h = h * (1.0 / torch.sqrt((h * h).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)) * self._norm_w().to(dev, dt)
    84	        base = h @ self.lm_head.T                          # [N,VOCAB]
    85	        bias = self._mw1(anchor.to(dev)).to(dev, dt) @ self._mw2_T(dt)  # [N,VOCAB]
    86	        return base + bias
    87	
    88	    def forward(self, x, anchor, temp=1.0):  # x [b,BLOCK,HC,DIM], anchor [b]
    89	        dev = x.device
    90	        dt = self.lm_head.dtype
    91	        h = self.hc_head(x.to(dev)).to(dev, dt)
    92	        h = h * (1.0 / torch.sqrt((h * h).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)) * self._norm_w().to(dev, dt)
    93	        base = h @ self.lm_head.T                                # [b, BLOCK, VOCAB]
    94	        b = x.shape[0]
    95	        out = torch.zeros(b, BLOCK + 1, dtype=torch.long, device=dev)
    96	        out[:, 0] = anchor.to(dev)
    97	        mw2_T = self._mw2_T(dt)                                  # [rank_m, VOCAB]
    98	        for i in range(BLOCK):
    99	            emb = self._mw1(out[:, i]).to(dev, dt)               # [b, rank_m]
   100	            li = (base[:, i] + emb @ mw2_T) / max(temp, 1e-5)
   101	            out[:, i + 1] = li.argmax(-1)
   102	        return out, base
   103	
   104	
   105	def build_head(dspark_path: str, target_path: str, device, lora_rank: int = 0,
   106	               dtype=torch.float32) -> DrafterHead:
   107	    _, T, _infos, _doff, _ = load_gguf_dense_only(dspark_path)
   108	    hc_fn = T["mtp.2.hc_head_fn.weight"][0]
   109	    hc_scale = T["mtp.2.hc_head_scale.weight"][0]
   110	    hc_base = T["mtp.2.hc_head_base.weight"][0]
   111	    norm_w = T["mtp.2.norm.weight"][0]
   112	    mw1 = T["mtp.2.markov_head.markov_w1.weight"][0]
   113	    mw2 = T["mtp.2.markov_head.markov_w2.weight"][0]
   114	    _, tinfos, tdoff = index_gguf(target_path)
   115	    lm_head = read_tensor(target_path, tinfos, tdoff, "output.weight").astype(np.float32)
   116	    head = DrafterHead(hc_fn, hc_scale, hc_base, norm_w, mw1, mw2, lm_head, lora_rank=lora_rank)
   117	    return head.to(device=device, dtype=dtype)

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 9 — final eval: McNemar paired test (frozen vs head-LoRA rank 32) + verdict.
     3	
     4	Trains the best head-LoRA config (rank 32) on train, computes per-anchor p=1
     5	predictions for the frozen head and the LoRA head on eval, runs a McNemar paired
     6	test, and records the reaffirm/not-justified verdict + localization diagnostics.
     7	"""
     8	from __future__ import annotations
     9	
    10	import json
    11	import sys
    12	from pathlib import Path
    13	
    14	import numpy as np
    15	import torch
    16	
    17	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    18	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_train"))
    19	from drafter_head import build_head   # noqa
    20	GGUF = HERE.parent.parent / "ds4" / "gguf"
    21	DSPARK = str((GGUF / "dspark.gguf").resolve())
    22	TARGET = str((GGUF / "DeepSeek-V4-Flash-IQ2XXS-w2Q2K-AProjQ8-SExpQ8-OutQ8-chat-v2-imatrix.gguf").resolve())
    23	DATA = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data"
    24	DEV = "mps"
    25	
    26	
    27	def load_features(split):
    28	    from safetensors import safe_open
    29	    f = DATA / f"{split}_torch_features.safetensors"
    30	    with safe_open(f, "numpy") as d:
    31	        return (torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("x").astype(np.float32)),
    32	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("anchor").astype(np.int64)),
    33	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("target_p1").astype(np.int64)))
    34	
    35	
    36	def preds(head, x, anchor, target, bs=256):
    37	    n = x.shape[0]; head.eval(); out = []
    38	    with torch.no_grad():
    39	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    40	            s = head.p1_scores(x[i:i+bs, 0], anchor[i:i+bs])
    41	            out.append(s.argmax(-1).cpu())
    42	    p = torch.cat(out)
    43	    return (p == target).numpy().astype(np.int8), p.numpy()
    44	
    45	
    46	def train(head, x, a, t, epochs=12, lr=3e-4, bs=256):
    47	    params = [p for p in head.parameters() if p.requires_grad]
    48	    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(params, lr=lr, weight_decay=0.01)
    49	    n = x.shape[0]; head.train()
    50	    for ep in range(epochs):
    51	        perm = torch.randperm(n)
    52	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    53	            idx = perm[i:i+bs]
    54	            loss = torch.nn.functional.cross_entropy(head.p1_scores(x[idx, 0], a[idx]), t[idx].to(DEV))
    55	            opt.zero_grad(); loss.backward(); opt.step()
    56	
    57	
    58	def mcnemar(pre, post):
    59	    b = int(((pre == 1) & (post == 0)).sum())  # pre-only-correct
    60	    c = int(((pre == 0) & (post == 1)).sum())  # post-only-correct
    61	    # exact 2-sided binomial on min(b,c) given b+c
    62	    from math import comb
    63	    tot = b + c; k = min(b, c)
    64	    if tot == 0:
    65	        return b, c, 1.0
    66	    p = 2 * sum(comb(tot, i) for i in range(0, k + 1)) * 0.5 ** tot
    67	    return b, c, min(1.0, p)
    68	
    69	
    70	def main():
    71	    xtr, atr, ttr = load_features("train"); xev, aev, tev = load_features("eval")
    72	    frozen = build_head(DSPARK, TARGET, device=DEV, lora_rank=0, dtype=torch.float32).eval()
    73	    pre, _ = preds(frozen, xev, aev, tev)
    74	    lora = build_head(DSPARK, TARGET, device=DEV, lora_rank=32, dtype=torch.float32)
    75	    train(lora, xtr, atr, ttr, epochs=12)
    76	    post, _ = preds(lora, xev, aev, tev)
    77	    pre_p1 = float(pre.mean()); post_p1 = float(post.mean())
    78	    b, c, pval = mcnemar(pre, post)
    79	    a6 = json.loads((DATA / "activity6_summary.json").read_text())
    80	    a7 = json.loads((DATA / "activity7_summary.json").read_text())
    81	    a4 = json.loads((HERE / "artifacts" / "stage2_crossed_oracle" / "summary.json").read_text())
    82	    delta_pp = (post_p1 - pre_p1) * 100
    83	    if delta_pp >= 5:
    84	        verdict = "REAFFIRM"
    85	    elif delta_pp < 1:
    86	        verdict = "NOT-JUSTIFIED"
    87	    else:
    88	        verdict = "INCONCLUSIVE"
    89	    print("=== Activity 9 FINAL EVAL ===")
    90	    print(f"  no-LoRA baseline (frozen head) p=1: {pre_p1:.4f}")
    91	    print(f"  head-LoRA rank32 p=1:              {post_p1:.4f}  (delta {delta_pp:+.2f} pp)")
    92	    print(f"  McNemar discordants: pre-only {b}, post-only {c}; p={pval:.4f}")
    93	    print(f"  rank sweep (A7) best delta: {a7['best_delta_pp']:+.2f} pp (flat across rank)")
    94	    print(f"  from-scratch ceiling (A6): {a6['head_only_ceiling_p1']:.4f}; overfit-to-eval {a6['overfit_to_eval_p1']:.4f}")
    95	    print(f"  crossed oracle (A4): input_effect {a4['aligned_cells']['input_effect']}, input_shift_rate {a4['input_shift_rate']}")
    96	    print(f"  Activity 8: skipped (7>6ceiling; A4 input-negligible)")
    97	    print(f"  VERDICT: {verdict}")
    98	    out = {
    99	        "no_lora_baseline_p1": pre_p1, "head_lora_rank32_p1": post_p1, "delta_pp": delta_pp,
   100	        "mcnemar_pre_only": b, "mcnemar_post_only": c, "mcnemar_p": pval,
   101	        "rank_sweep_best_delta_pp": a7["best_delta_pp"], "from_scratch_ceiling_p1": a6["head_only_ceiling_p1"],
   102	        "overfit_to_eval_p1": a6["overfit_to_eval_p1"], "crossed_input_effect": a4["aligned_cells"]["input_effect"],
   103	        "crossed_input_shift_rate": a4["input_shift_rate"], "activity8": "skipped (7>6ceiling; A4 input-negligible)",

   108	                            "input-invariant). Consistent with Stage 0/1 (deficit is shallow but not head-fixable "
   109	                            "via non-expert LoRA; raising tap precision didn't help). Expert tuning is out of scope.")}
   110	    (DATA / "activity9_final.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 6 — from-scratch head upper bound [GATE] (RESEARCH INSTRUMENTATION).
     3	
     4	Trains a from-scratch head (hc_head + norm + markov_w1/w2 trainable; lm_head frozen)
     5	on the frozen pre-head features x (torch MPS body) to predict the p=1 target, and
     6	reports the held-out p=1 ceiling. Also reports (a) the frozen pretrained head's p=1
     7	(the no-LoRA baseline / 'pre-') and (b) an overfit-to-eval check (train on eval ->
     8	p=1 ~1.0 proves the head/impl can fit). Gate decision: high ceiling (~0.88-0.91) =>
     9	h carries the info, proceed to 7; caps low (~0.83) => h lacks info, move toward 8.
    10	"""
    11	from __future__ import annotations
    12	
    13	import sys
    14	from pathlib import Path
    15	
    16	import numpy as np
    17	import torch
    18	import torch.nn as nn
    19	
    20	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    21	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_train"))
    22	from drafter_head import build_head, HC, DIM, NORM_EPS, HC_EPS  # noqa
    23	GGUF = HERE.parent.parent / "ds4" / "gguf"
    24	DSPARK = str((GGUF / "dspark.gguf").resolve())
    25	TARGET = str((GGUF / "DeepSeek-V4-Flash-IQ2XXS-w2Q2K-AProjQ8-SExpQ8-OutQ8-chat-v2-imatrix.gguf").resolve())
    26	DATA = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data"
    27	DEV = "mps"
    28	
    29	
    30	def load_features(split):
    31	    from safetensors import safe_open
    32	    f = DATA / f"{split}_torch_features.safetensors"
    33	    with safe_open(f, "numpy") as d:
    34	        return (torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("x").astype(np.float32)),
    35	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("anchor").astype(np.int64)),
    36	                torch.from_numpy(d.get_tensor("target_p1").astype(np.int64)))
    37	
    38	
    39	class FromScratchHead(nn.Module):
    40	    def __init__(self, lm_head):
    41	        super().__init__()
    42	        self.register_buffer("lm_head", lm_head)
    43	        self.hc_fn = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(HC, HC * DIM))
    44	        self.hc_scale = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(1))
    45	        self.hc_base = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(HC))
    46	        self.norm_w = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(DIM))
    47	        self.markov_w1 = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(129280, 256))
    48	        self.markov_w2 = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(129280, 256))
    49	        nn.init.normal_(self.hc_fn, std=0.02)
    50	        nn.init.normal_(self.markov_w1, std=0.02)
    51	        nn.init.normal_(self.markov_w2, std=0.02)
    52	
    53	    def p1_scores(self, x0, anchor):  # x0 [N,HC,DIM], anchor [N] -> [N,VOCAB]
    54	        flat = x0.reshape(x0.shape[0], HC * DIM)
    55	        rsqrt = torch.rsqrt((flat * flat).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)
    56	        mixes = (flat @ self.hc_fn.T) * rsqrt
    57	        pre = torch.sigmoid(mixes * self.hc_scale[0] + self.hc_base) + HC_EPS
    58	        h = (pre.unsqueeze(-1) * x0).sum(1)
    59	        h = h * torch.rsqrt((h * h).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS) * self.norm_w
    60	        base = h @ self.lm_head.T
    61	        bias = self.markov_w1[anchor] @ self.markov_w2.T
    62	        return base + bias
    63	
    64	
    65	def p1_acc(head, x, anchor, target, bs=256):
    66	    n = x.shape[0]; match = 0
    67	    head.eval()
    68	    with torch.no_grad():
    69	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    70	            xb = x[i:i+bs].to(DEV); ab = anchor[i:i+bs].to(DEV)
    71	            if hasattr(head, "p1_scores"):
    72	                pred = head.p1_scores(xb[:, 0], ab).argmax(-1)
    73	            else:  # DrafterHead (frozen) -> full rollout, take p=1
    74	                out, _ = head(xb, ab); pred = out[:, 1]
    75	            match += (pred.cpu() == target[i:i+bs]).sum().item()
    76	    return match / n
    77	
    78	
    79	def train_head(head, x, anchor, target, epochs=8, lr=1e-3, bs=256, tag=""):
    80	    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(head.parameters(), lr=lr, weight_decay=0.01)
    81	    n = x.shape[0]; head.train()
    82	    for ep in range(epochs):
    83	        perm = torch.randperm(n); tl = 0.0
    84	        for i in range(0, n, bs):
    85	            idx = perm[i:i+bs]
    86	            s = head.p1_scores(x[idx, 0].to(DEV), anchor[idx].to(DEV))
    87	            loss = torch.nn.functional.cross_entropy(s, target[idx].to(DEV))
    88	            opt.zero_grad(); loss.backward(); opt.step(); tl += loss.item() * len(idx)
    89	        print(f"  {tag}epoch {ep+1}: train CE {tl/n:.4f}", flush=True)
    90	
    91	
    92	def main():
    93	    print("loading features...", flush=True)
    94	    xtr, atr, ttr = load_features("train"); xev, aev, tev = load_features("eval")
    95	    print(f"train {xtr.shape[0]} eval {xev.shape[0]} anchors", flush=True)
    96	    # frozen pretrained head baseline (the 'pre-')
    97	    print("frozen pretrained head (no-LoRA baseline)...", flush=True)
    98	    frozen = build_head(DSPARK, TARGET, device=DEV, lora_rank=0, dtype=torch.float32).eval()
    99	    # reuse its lm_head for the from-scratch head
   100	    lm_head = frozen.lm_head
   101	    base_p1 = p1_acc(frozen, xev, aev, tev)
   102	    print(f"  frozen-head p=1 on eval (no-LoRA baseline / pre-): {base_p1:.4f}", flush=True)
   103	
   104	    # from-scratch head ceiling
   105	    print("training from-scratch head on train...", flush=True)
   106	    fs = FromScratchHead(lm_head.to(DEV)).to(DEV)
   107	    train_head(fs, xtr, atr, ttr, epochs=25, lr=2e-3, tag="train ")
   108	    ceil = p1_acc(fs, xev, aev, tev)
   109	    print(f"  from-scratch head p=1 on eval (HEAD-ONLY CEILING): {ceil:.4f}", flush=True)
   110	
   111	    # overfit-to-eval sanity check
   112	    print("overfit-to-eval check (train on eval)...", flush=True)
   113	    fs2 = FromScratchHead(lm_head.to(DEV)).to(DEV)
   114	    train_head(fs2, xev, aev, tev, epochs=60, lr=2e-3, tag="overfit ")
   115	    oe = p1_acc(fs2, xev, aev, tev)
   116	    print(f"  overfit-to-eval p=1: {oe:.4f} (want ~1.0 -> head can fit)", flush=True)
   117	
   118	    print(f"\n=== Activity 6 SUMMARY ===")
   119	    print(f"  no-LoRA baseline (frozen head):   {base_p1:.4f}")
   120	    print(f"  head-only ceiling (from-scratch): {ceil:.4f}")
   121	    print(f"  overfit-to-eval:                  {oe:.4f}")
   122	    gate = "proceed to 7 (LoRA)" if ceil >= base_p1 + 0.03 else "h lacks info -> consider 8"
   123	    print(f"  gate: {gate}")
   124	    import json
   125	    (DATA / "activity6_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps({
   126	        "no_lora_baseline_p1": base_p1, "head_only_ceiling_p1": ceil,
   127	        "overfit_to_eval_p1": oe, "gate": gate}, indent=2) + "\n")
   128	
   129	
   130	if __name__ == "__main__":
   131	    main()

codex
The head LoRA implementation has a severe red flag: every low-rank factor is initialized to zero, so for products like `B @ A`, both sides receive exactly zero gradient on the first step and remain dead. I’m going to verify that directly with a small gradient/progress check on one batch.

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba issue468/dspark_train/drafter_body.py | sed -n '1,260p'" in /Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 5 — DSpark drafter BODY in torch (MPS-capable), RESEARCH INSTRUMENTATION.
     3	
     4	Faithful port of dspark_oracle/measure_acceptance_bundle's forward loop (main_proj +
     5	3 DSpark blocks: hc_pre -> MLA attention -> hc_post -> hc_pre -> MoE -> hc_post),
     6	producing the pre-head feature x [BLOCK, HC, DIM] per anchor with the window KV cache.
     7	Routed experts dequanted (Q4_K) to F16 and stacked per layer for vectorized topk
     8	dispatch. Frozen (Activity 5 fidelity); Activity 8 adds body LoRA on top.
     9	
    10	Body fidelity gate (run_body_fidelity.py): torch x vs numpy x on the exactness corpus.
    11	The head module (drafter_head.py) is separate and already fidelity-verified.
    12	"""
    13	from __future__ import annotations
    14	
    15	import sys
    16	from pathlib import Path
    17	
    18	import numpy as np
    19	import torch
    20	
    21	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent            # issue468/
    22	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_oracle"))
    23	from gguf_loader import load_gguf_dense_only, index_gguf, read_tensor, dequant_q4_k_expert  # noqa: E402
    24	from measure_acceptance_bundle import layer_weights       # noqa: E402  (exact key mapping)
    25	
    26	HC, DIM, BLOCK = 4, 4096, 5
    27	HEAD_DIM, ROPE_DIM, N_HEADS, N_GROUPS, O_LORA = 512, 64, 64, 8, 1024
    28	WIN, TOPK, ROUTE_SCALE, SWIGLU_LIMIT = 128, 6, 1.5, 10.0
    29	NORM_EPS, HC_EPS = 1e-6, 1e-6
    30	NOISE_TOK = 128799
    31	
    32	
    33	def rmsnorm(x, w, eps=NORM_EPS):
    34	    return x * torch.rsqrt((x * x).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + eps) * w
    35	
    36	
    37	def apply_rotary(x_last, cos, sin):
    38	    xe = x_last[..., 0::2]; xo = x_last[..., 1::2]
    39	    out = torch.empty_like(x_last)
    40	    out[..., 0::2] = xe * cos - xo * sin
    41	    out[..., 1::2] = xe * sin + xo * cos
    42	    return out
    43	
    44	
    45	def hc_split_sinkhorn(mixes, scale, base, hc=HC, iters=20, eps=HC_EPS):
    46	    pre = torch.sigmoid(mixes[..., :hc] * scale[0] + base[:hc]) + eps
    47	    post = 2.0 * torch.sigmoid(mixes[..., hc:2 * hc] * scale[1] + base[hc:2 * hc])
    48	    comb = (mixes[..., 2 * hc:] * scale[2] + base[2 * hc:]).reshape(*mixes.shape[:-1], hc, hc)
    49	    comb = comb - comb.amax(-1, keepdim=True); comb = torch.exp(comb)
    50	    comb = comb / comb.sum(-1, keepdim=True) + eps
    51	    col = comb.sum(-2, keepdim=True); comb = comb / (col + eps)
    52	    for _ in range(iters - 1):
    53	        comb = comb / (comb.sum(-1, keepdim=True) + eps)
    54	        comb = comb / (comb.sum(-2, keepdim=True) + eps)
    55	    return pre, post, comb
    56	
    57	
    58	def hc_pre(x_hc, fn, scale, base):
    59	    b, s, hc, d = x_hc.shape
    60	    flat = x_hc.reshape(b, s, hc * d)
    61	    rsqrt = torch.rsqrt((flat * flat).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)
    62	    pre, post, comb = hc_split_sinkhorn((flat @ fn.T) * rsqrt, scale, base, hc)
    63	    return (pre.unsqueeze(-1) * x_hc).sum(2), post, comb
    64	
    65	
    66	def hc_post(x, residual, post, comb):
    67	    return post.unsqueeze(-1) * x.unsqueeze(-2) + (comb.unsqueeze(-1) * residual.unsqueeze(-3)).sum(-2)
    68	
    69	
    70	def sparse_attn(q, kv_g, sink, scale):
    71	    scores = torch.einsum("bshd,bstd->bsht", q, kv_g) * scale
    72	    m = scores.amax(-1)                                     # [b,s,h]
    73	    sink_t = torch.maximum(m, sink.view(1, 1, -1))          # [b,s,h]
    74	    numer = torch.exp(scores - sink_t.unsqueeze(-1))        # [b,s,h,t]
    75	    denom = numer.sum(-1) + torch.exp(sink.view(1, 1, -1) - sink_t)  # [b,s,h]
    76	    return torch.einsum("bsht,bstd->bshd", numer, kv_g) / denom.unsqueeze(-1)
    77	
    78	
    79	def softplus(x):
    80	    return torch.where(x > 20, x, torch.log1p(torch.exp(torch.clamp(x, max=20))))
    81	
    82	
    83	class DrafterBody:
    84	    """Frozen drafter body (plain tensors). forward_prompt returns x per anchor."""
    85	    def __init__(self, p, dev, dt):
    86	        self.dev, self.dt = dev, dt
    87	        g = lambda k: torch.as_tensor(np.ascontiguousarray(p[k]), device=dev, dtype=dt)
    88	        self.main_proj = g("main_proj"); self.main_norm = g("main_norm")
    89	        self.embed_w = g("embed_w"); self.cos = g("cos"); self.sin = g("sin")
    90	        self.layers = [{k: g(f"L{s}_{k}") for k in p["layer_keys"]} for s in range(3)]
    91	        self.exp_gate = g("exp_gate"); self.exp_up = g("exp_up"); self.exp_down = g("exp_down")
    92	
    93	    def _moe(self, x, s):
    94	        w = self.layers[s]
    95	        xf = x.reshape(-1, DIM)
    96	        scores = torch.sqrt(softplus(xf @ w["ffn_gate_inp"].T))
    97	        sel = scores + w["ffn_exp_probs_b"]
    98	        idx = sel.topk(TOPK, dim=-1).indices
    99	        wg = torch.gather(scores, -1, idx); wg = wg / (wg.sum(-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-12) * ROUTE_SCALE
   100	        y = torch.zeros_like(xf)
   101	        for k in range(TOPK):
   102	            ids = idx[:, k]
   103	            gg = torch.bmm(xf.unsqueeze(1), self.exp_gate[s][ids].transpose(1, 2)).squeeze(1)
   104	            uu = torch.bmm(xf.unsqueeze(1), self.exp_up[s][ids].transpose(1, 2)).squeeze(1)
   105	            gg = torch.clamp(gg, max=SWIGLU_LIMIT); uu = torch.clamp(uu, -SWIGLU_LIMIT, SWIGLU_LIMIT)
   106	            inter = (gg * torch.sigmoid(gg)) * uu
   107	            out = torch.bmm(inter.unsqueeze(1), self.exp_down[s][ids].transpose(1, 2)).squeeze(1)
   108	            y = y + wg[:, k:k + 1] * out
   109	        g0 = xf @ w["ffn_gate_shexp"].T; u0 = xf @ w["ffn_up_shexp"].T
   110	        g0 = torch.clamp(g0, max=SWIGLU_LIMIT); u0 = torch.clamp(u0, -SWIGLU_LIMIT, SWIGLU_LIMIT)
   111	        y = y + (g0 * torch.sigmoid(g0)) * u0 @ w["ffn_down_shexp"].T
   112	        return y.reshape(1, x.shape[1], DIM)
   113	
   114	    def _attn(self, x_draft, win_kv, n_real, s, step):
   115	        w = self.layers[s]; block = x_draft.shape[1]; scale = HEAD_DIM ** -0.5
   116	        qr = rmsnorm(x_draft @ w["q_a"].T, w["q_a_norm"])
   117	        q = (qr @ w["q_b"].T).reshape(1, block, N_HEADS, HEAD_DIM)
   118	        q = q * torch.rsqrt((q * q).mean(-1, keepdim=True) + NORM_EPS)
   119	        cs = self.cos[step + 1:step + 1 + block][None, :, None, :]   # [1,block,1,32] for q/o
   120	        ss = self.sin[step + 1:step + 1 + block][None, :, None, :]
   121	        cs_kv = self.cos[step + 1:step + 1 + block][None, :, :]        # [1,block,32] for kv
   122	        ss_kv = self.sin[step + 1:step + 1 + block][None, :, :]
   123	        q[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(q[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs, ss)
   124	        kv = rmsnorm(x_draft @ w["kv"].T, w["kv_a_norm"])
   125	        kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs_kv, ss_kv)
   126	        kv_all = torch.cat([win_kv.unsqueeze(0), kv], dim=1)
   127	        idx = torch.cat([torch.arange(n_real, device=self.dev),
   128	                         torch.arange(WIN, WIN + block, device=self.dev)])
   129	        kv_g = kv_all[:, idx, :].unsqueeze(1).expand(1, block, idx.size(0), HEAD_DIM)
   130	        o = sparse_attn(q, kv_g, w["attn_sinks"], scale)
   131	        o[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(o[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs, -ss)
   132	        gd = HEAD_DIM * N_HEADS // N_GROUPS
   133	        wo_a = w["output_a"].reshape(N_GROUPS, O_LORA, gd)
   134	        o_lor = torch.einsum("bsgd,grd->bsgr", o.reshape(1, block, N_GROUPS, gd), wo_a)
   135	        return (o_lor.reshape(1, block, N_GROUPS * O_LORA) @ w["output_b"].T).to(self.dt)
   136	
   137	    def forward_prompt(self, main_hidden_seq, anchors, return_layers=False):
   138	        dev, dt = self.dev, self.dt
   139	        mh = torch.as_tensor(np.asarray(main_hidden_seq, np.float32), device=dev, dtype=dt)
   140	        win_kv = [torch.zeros(WIN, HEAD_DIM, device=dev, dtype=dt) for _ in range(3)]
   141	        main_x0 = rmsnorm(mh[0:1].reshape(1, 1, 3 * DIM) @ self.main_proj.T, self.main_norm)
   142	        for s in range(3):
   143	            mkv = rmsnorm(main_x0 @ self.layers[s]["kv"].T, self.layers[s]["kv_a_norm"])
   144	            mkv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(mkv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], self.cos[0], self.sin[0])
   145	            win_kv[s][0] = mkv[0, 0]
   146	        n_real = 1; xs = []; layer_xs = []
   147	        for step in range(1, len(anchors) + 1):
   148	            main_x = rmsnorm(mh[step:step + 1].reshape(1, 1, 3 * DIM) @ self.main_proj.T, self.main_norm)
   149	            for s in range(3):
   150	                mkv = rmsnorm(main_x @ self.layers[s]["kv"].T, self.layers[s]["kv_a_norm"])
   151	                mkv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(mkv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], self.cos[step], self.sin[step])
   152	                win_kv[s][step % WIN] = mkv[0, 0]
   153	            n_real = min(n_real + 1, WIN)
   154	            draft_ids = torch.full((BLOCK,), NOISE_TOK, dtype=torch.long, device=dev); draft_ids[0] = int(anchors[step - 1])
   155	            x = self.embed_w[draft_ids][None, :, None, :].expand(1, BLOCK, HC, DIM).clone()
   156	            step_layers = []
   157	            for s in range(3):
   158	                w = self.layers[s]
   159	                res = x
   160	                yd, post, comb = hc_pre(x, w["hc_attn_fn"], w["hc_attn_scale"], w["hc_attn_base"])
   161	                x = hc_post(self._attn(rmsnorm(yd, w["attn_norm"]), win_kv[s], n_real, s, step), res, post, comb)
   162	                res = x
   163	                yd, post, comb = hc_pre(x, w["hc_ffn_fn"], w["hc_ffn_scale"], w["hc_ffn_base"])
   164	                x = hc_post(self._moe(rmsnorm(yd, w["ffn_norm"]), s), res, post, comb)
   165	                if return_layers: step_layers.append(x[0].clone())
   166	            xs.append(x[0])
   167	            if return_layers: layer_xs.append(torch.stack(step_layers))  # [3,BLOCK,HC,DIM]
   168	        if return_layers:
   169	            return torch.stack(xs), torch.stack(layer_xs)  # [n,..], [n,3,..]
   170	        return torch.stack(xs)
   171	
   172	
   173	def build_body(dspark_path, target_path, device, dtype=torch.float32):
   174	    cache = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data" / "drafter_body_weights.npz"
   175	    if cache.exists():
   176	        lo = np.load(cache, allow_pickle=True)
   177	        p = {k: lo[k] for k in lo.files}
   178	        p["layer_keys"] = ["hc_attn_fn", "hc_attn_scale", "hc_attn_base", "attn_norm",
   179	                  "q_a", "q_a_norm", "q_b", "kv", "kv_a_norm", "attn_sinks",
   180	                  "output_a", "output_b", "hc_ffn_fn", "hc_ffn_scale", "hc_ffn_base",
   181	                  "ffn_norm", "ffn_gate_inp", "ffn_exp_probs_b", "ffn_gate_shexp",
   182	                  "ffn_up_shexp", "ffn_down_shexp"]
   183	        return DrafterBody(p, device, dtype)
   184	    _, T, infos, doff, _ = load_gguf_dense_only(dspark_path)
   185	    layer_keys = ["hc_attn_fn", "hc_attn_scale", "hc_attn_base", "attn_norm",
   186	                  "q_a", "q_a_norm", "q_b", "kv", "kv_a_norm", "attn_sinks",
   187	                  "output_a", "output_b", "hc_ffn_fn", "hc_ffn_scale", "hc_ffn_base",
   188	                  "ffn_norm", "ffn_gate_inp", "ffn_exp_probs_b", "ffn_gate_shexp",
   189	                  "ffn_up_shexp", "ffn_down_shexp"]
   190	    p = {"main_proj": T["mtp.0.main_proj.weight"][0], "main_norm": T["mtp.0.main_norm.weight"][0],
   191	         "layer_keys": layer_keys}
   192	    for s in range(3):
   193	        lw = layer_weights(T, s)
   194	        for k in layer_keys:
   195	            p[f"L{s}_{k}"] = lw[k]
   196	    eg = np.stack([np.stack([dequant_q4_k_expert(dspark_path, infos, doff, f"mtp.{s}.ffn_gate_exps.weight", e)
   197	                             for e in range(256)]) for s in range(3)])
   198	    eu = np.stack([np.stack([dequant_q4_k_expert(dspark_path, infos, doff, f"mtp.{s}.ffn_up_exps.weight", e)
   199	                             for e in range(256)]) for s in range(3)])
   200	    ed = np.stack([np.stack([dequant_q4_k_expert(dspark_path, infos, doff, f"mtp.{s}.ffn_down_exps.weight", e)
   201	                             for e in range(256)]) for s in range(3)])
   202	    p["exp_gate"] = eg; p["exp_up"] = eu; p["exp_down"] = ed
   203	    _, tinfos, tdoff = index_gguf(target_path)
   204	    p["embed_w"] = read_tensor(target_path, tinfos, tdoff, "token_embd.weight").astype(np.float32)
   205	    half = ROPE_DIM // 2
   206	    freqs = 1.0 / (10000.0 ** (np.arange(0, ROPE_DIM, 2, dtype=np.float64) / ROPE_DIM))
   207	    ang = np.outer(np.arange(4096, dtype=np.float64), freqs)
   208	    p["cos"] = np.cos(ang).astype(np.float32); p["sin"] = np.sin(ang).astype(np.float32)
   209	    save = {k: v for k, v in p.items() if isinstance(v, np.ndarray)}
   210	    np.savez(cache, **save)
   211	    return DrafterBody(p, device, dtype)

the hidden state at the first position. Regardless of cause, the practical signal is
clear.

## Implication for the LoRA focus

- **Output side (hc_head + markov) is the lever** — train the ordering to match the
  served target's argmax. **Strongly supports Activity 6/7 (head-only) as the primary
  intervention.**
- **Input side (main_proj + dense) is unlikely to help** — the drafter already ignores
  the hidden-state perturbation, so remapping the representation would add little.
  This downweights the expected value of Activity 8's input-side ablations (still run
  them if 7 < 6, but expect a small incremental gain).

Net: the crossed oracle predicts the head-only LoRA (Activity 7) is where any
acceptance gain will come from, and that the from-scratch head ceiling (Activity 6)
is the gate to watch. Consistent with Stage 0 (shallow, output-ordering errors) and
Stage 1 (raising input precision didn't help).

 succeeded in 0ms:
- **Activity 2 — Build `--capture-dataset` + multi-layer patch + golden-parity test**
  (engine repo; research instrumentation).
- **Activity 3 — Capture data → sharded safetensors** (~13 min; 180 prompts × 128 tok).
- **Activity 4 — Crossed hidden/label oracle (mandatory).** From existing IQ2XXS +
  Q4-tap captures: Q4-hidden + IQ2XXS-tokens and IQ2XXS-hidden + Q4-tokens, re-measure.
  Separates input-shift (representation) from label-shift (argmax drift) *before*
  training and tells the LoRA where to focus.
- **Activity 5 — Torch port + fidelity gate.** Port `dspark_oracle/forward.py`→torch
  (MPS); experts dequant to F16 frozen. **Gate: torch vs numpy full-score + top-128
  parity (not argmax-only)** on the exactness corpus; no-LoRA eval reproduces baseline p=1.
- **Activity 6 — From-scratch head upper bound [gate before LoRA].** Train a
  from-scratch head (hc_head+markov, full-rank) on the frozen pre-head features `h` to
  convergence; report held-out p=1. This measures the **true head-only ceiling**
  directly — the Stage-0 top-2 coverage (~0.91) is only a loose indicator, not a
  ceiling, since a retrained head produces a different distribution and could move the
  target from rank-5→1 if `h` carries the info. If it caps low (~0.83), `h` lacks the
  info ⇒ the deficit is upstream of the head ⇒ move toward Activity 8. If it is high
  (~0.90) but Activity 7 is low, LoRA rank is the bottleneck.
- **Activity 7 — Head-only LoRA** (hc_head + markov + norm; rank 32; cheap; compare to
  the Activity 6 ceiling).
- **Activity 8 — Full-drafter LoRA** if Activity 7 < Activity 6 ceiling (add main_proj
  + dense attn/FFN-shexp). **Ablate to isolate input-side** — head-only /
  main_proj-only / dense-block-only / head+main_proj / head+blocks at matched rank —
  so the gain isn't confounded with extra capacity. Rank 32, then 64/128 rescue if needed.
- **Activity 9 — Eval + decision.** McNemar paired p=1; apply the decision rule.

## Storage format

Sharded **safetensors**. Per shard: `main_hidden [N,12288] f16`; `token_ids [N] i32`
(target greedy stream; K-step targets via windowing); `topk_ids [N,128] i32` +
**`topk_logprobs [N,128] f16`** (+ optional `topk_logits`) for Ltv; `selected_id [N]
i32`; `prompt_idx [N] i32`; `pos_in_prompt [N] i32`. Plus `index.json` (prompt→source,
text ref, prompt_tokens). **Explicit no-cross-prompt windowing** at train time. Layout:
train ~4 shards, eval 1 shard + index.

## Sanity / fidelity checks

issue468/ref/DSpark_paper.md:147:To address this, we introduce **Sequential Temperature Scaling (STS)** . Because each _𝑐𝑖_ models a conditional probability, the chain rule dictates that the joint probability of a draft prefix being accepted factorizes into the cumulative product[�] _𝑖_ ⩽ _𝑘[𝑐][𝑖]_[.][Using a held-out validation set, STS] calibrates this joint probability consecutively from left to right. Specifically, at each position _𝑘_ ∈{1, _. . ._ , _𝛾_ }, we perform a simple 1D grid search to find the optimal temperature scalar that minimizes the Expected Calibration Error (ECE) (Naeini et al., 2015) of the cumulative product, keeping the already-calibrated scores of all preceding positions fixed. Crucially, temperature scaling is an order-preserving transformation: it rectifies the predicted probabilities to match empirical acceptance rates without disrupting the relative draft token rankings learned by the confidence head. 
issue468/ref/DSpark_paper.md:203:**Training data.** We use Open-PerfectBlend[2] , an open-sourced version of PerfectBlend (Xu et al., 2024) consisting of 1.3 million samples. It is a general-purpose instruction dataset containing chat (17.6%), math (39.4%), code (38.9%), and instruction-following data (4.1%). We only use the prompts from Open-PerfectBlend; responses are regenerated by each target model with recommended sampling parameters. Each drafter is trained for 10 epochs to ensure full convergence. For data generation and evaluation, we adopt the non-thinking mode.

issue468/ref/DSpark_paper.md:310:**From Static Thresholds to Calibrated Scheduling.** While useful for diagnostics, a static threshold is sub-optimal in dynamic serving environments because it ignores system load: verifying low-confidence tokens incurs minimal opportunity cost under low concurrency, but wastes critical batch capacity under high concurrency. This load dependency motivates the hardware-aware prefix scheduler. As formulated in Section 3.2, maximizing system-level throughput requires the confidence model to exhibit both strong predictive discrimination and precise calibration to accurately estimate cumulative survival probabilities. The reliability diagram (Figure 6) demonstrates that while the raw model achieves strong discrimination (ROC-AUC (Hanley and McNeil, 1982) ranging from 0.81 to 0.90), it is overly confident (ECE 3%–8%). Applying post-hoc STS (Section 3.2.1) mitigates this overconfidence, reducing the ∼ average ECE to 1% and yielding reliable survival estimates.

issue468/artifacts/quant_mismatch_diagnostic/codex_review_output.txt:4848:To address this, we introduce **Sequential Temperature Scaling (STS)** . Because each _𝑐𝑖_ models a conditional probability, the chain rule dictates that the joint probability of a draft prefix being accepted factorizes into the cumulative product[�] _𝑖_ ⩽ _𝑘[𝑐][𝑖]_[.][Using a held-out validation set, STS] calibrates this joint probability consecutively from left to right. Specifically, at each position _𝑘_ ∈{1, _. . ._ , _𝛾_ }, we perform a simple 1D grid search to find the optimal temperature scalar that minimizes the Expected Calibration Error (ECE) (Naeini et al., 2015) of the cumulative product, keeping the already-calibrated scores of all preceding positions fixed. Crucially, temperature scaling is an order-preserving transformation: it rectifies the predicted probabilities to match empirical acceptance rates without disrupting the relative draft token rankings learned by the confidence head. 
issue468/artifacts/quant_mismatch_diagnostic/codex_review_output.txt:4904:**Training data.** We use Open-PerfectBlend[2] , an open-sourced version of PerfectBlend (Xu et al., 2024) consisting of 1.3 million samples. It is a general-purpose instruction dataset containing chat (17.6%), math (39.4%), code (38.9%), and instruction-following data (4.1%). We only use the prompts from Open-PerfectBlend; responses are regenerated by each target model with recommended sampling parameters. Each drafter is trained for 10 epochs to ensure full convergence. For data generation and evaluation, we adopt the non-thinking mode.

Crucially, temperature scaling is an order-preserving transformation: it rectifies the predicted probabilities to match empirical acceptance rates without disrupting the relative draft token rankings learned by the confidence head.

issue468/artifacts/quant_mismatch_diagnostic/codex_review_output.txt:5012:310:**From Static Thresholds to Calibrated Scheduling.** While useful for diagnostics, a static threshold is sub-optimal in dynamic serving environments because it ignores system load: verifying low-confidence tokens incurs minimal opportunity cost under low concurrency, but wastes critical batch capacity under high concurrency. This load dependency motivates the hardware-aware prefix scheduler. As formulated in Section 3.2, maximizing system-level throughput requires the confidence model to exhibit both strong predictive discrimination and precise calibration to accurately estimate cumulative survival probabilities. The reliability diagram (Figure 6) demonstrates that while the raw model achieves strong discrimination (ROC-AUC (Hanley and McNeil, 1982) ranging from 0.81 to 0.90), it is overly confident (ECE 3%–8%). Applying post-hoc STS (Section 3.2.1) mitigates this overconfidence, reducing the ∼ average ECE to 1% and yielding reliable survival estimates.

issue468/artifacts/quant_mismatch_diagnostic/codex_review_output.txt:5197:**From Static Thresholds to Calibrated Scheduling.** While useful for diagnostics, a static threshold is sub-optimal in dynamic serving environments because it ignores system load: verifying low-confidence tokens incurs minimal opportunity cost under low concurrency, but wastes critical batch capacity under high concurrency. This load dependency motivates the hardware-aware prefix scheduler. As formulated in Section 3.2, maximizing system-level throughput requires the confidence model to exhibit both strong predictive discrimination and precise calibration to accurately estimate cumulative survival probabilities. The reliability diagram (Figure 6) demonstrates that while the raw model achieves strong discrimination (ROC-AUC (Hanley and McNeil, 1982) ranging from 0.81 to 0.90), it is overly confident (ECE 3%–8%). Applying post-hoc STS (Section 3.2.1) mitigates this overconfidence, reducing the ∼ average ECE to 1% and yielding reliable survival estimates.

issue468/artifacts/stage2_plan_review/codex_review_output.txt:4213:   203	**Training data.** We use Open-PerfectBlend[2] , an open-sourced version of PerfectBlend (Xu et al., 2024) consisting of 1.3 million samples. It is a general-purpose instruction dataset containing chat (17.6%), math (39.4%), code (38.9%), and instruction-following data (4.1%). We only use the prompts from Open-PerfectBlend; responses are regenerated by each target model with recommended sampling parameters. Each drafter is trained for 10 epochs to ensure full convergence. For data generation and evaluation, we adopt the non-thinking mode. 
issue468/artifacts/stage2_plan_review/codex_review_output.txt:4288:147:To address this, we introduce **Sequential Temperature Scaling (STS)** . Because each _𝑐𝑖_ models a conditional probability, the chain rule dictates that the joint probability of a draft prefix being accepted factorizes into the cumulative product[�] _𝑖_ ⩽ _𝑘[𝑐][𝑖]_[.][Using a held-out validation set, STS] calibrates this joint probability consecutively from left to right. Specifically, at each position _𝑘_ ∈{1, _. . ._ , _𝛾_ }, we perform a simple 1D grid search to find the optimal temperature scalar that minimizes the Expected Calibration Error (ECE) (Naeini et al., 2015) of the cumulative product, keeping the already-calibrated scores of all preceding positions fixed. Crucially, temperature scaling is an order-preserving transformation: it rectifies the predicted probabilities to match empirical acceptance rates without disrupting the relative draft token rankings learned by the confidence head.

A threshold of 0 corresponds to standard fixed-length<br>verification. As the threshold increases, the overall acceptance rate steadily rises because the<br>confidence head effectively prunes tokens that would ultimately be rejected (hashed bars).<br>Perfect calibration Before calibration After calibration<br>Position 1 Position 3 Position 5 Position 7<br>1.00 ECE: 5.7%   2.0% ECE: 8.2%   1.7% ECE: 5.8%   0.8% ECE: 3.3%   0.4%<br>AUC: 0.818 AUC: 0.812 AUC: 0.864 AUC: 0.907<br>0.75<br>0.50<br>0.25<br>0.00<br>0.00 0.25 0.50 0.75 1.00 0.00 0.25 0.50 0.75 1.00 0.00 0.25 0.50 0.75 1.00 0.00 0.25 0.50 0.75 1.00<br>Predicted Acceptance<br>Acceptance Rate<br>Avg. # dspark_train — Stage 2 drafter fine-tune PoC (RESEARCH INSTRUMENTATION)

PyTorch (MPS) environment for the bounded drafter fine-tune described in
`issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md`. Not part of the ds4 engine or
the numpy oracle; this is the Stage-2 training/eval harness.

## Setup

```bash
cd issue468/dspark_train
python3 -m venv .venv            # CPython 3.14 (torch cp314 wheel includes MPS)
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

Verify MPS:

```bash
python -c "import torch; print(torch.backends.mps.is_available())"   # True
```

## Scope

- Torch port of the drafter forward (reference: `issue468/dspark_oracle/forward.py`),
  with a fidelity gate (LoRA=0 must reproduce the numpy oracle's draft tokens).
- LoRA fine-tune: Stage 2a head-only (precomputed features), Stage 2b full-drafter.
- Frozen routed MoE experts (dequant to F16) + the target's shared embed/lm_head.
- Reads sharded capture data (few-file, safetensors/.npz) produced by the
  `--capture-dataset` ds4 mode (see the protocol note, Phase A).

## Notes

- The `.venv/` is gitignored (see `issue468/.gitignore`). Do not commit checkpoints
  or large capture shards — keep them gitignored and reference them in
  `issue468/STATUS.md` / inventories.
- 128 GB unified memory on the target machine: the ~23 GB F16 drafter experts,
  activations, and capture shards all fit comfortably.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Activity 6/7/9 prep — extract pre-head features x via the torch MPS drafter body.
     3	
     4	Self-consistent feature source for head training/eval. For each prompt in a split,
     5	runs DrafterBody.forward_prompt over its generated positions, collecting per anchor:
     6	  x [BLOCK,HC,DIM] f16   (pre-head feature)
     7	  anchor i64             (token at the anchor position)
     8	  target_p1 i64          (the next token = the p=1 target)
     9	Alignment: anchor = token_ids[s], target_p1 = token_ids[s+1], s=1..G-2 (n=G-2 anchors);
    10	mh_seq = main_hidden[0:G-1]. Saves {split}_torch_features.safetensors.
    11	"""
    12	from __future__ import annotations
    13	
    14	import argparse
    15	import json
    16	import sys
    17	import time
    18	from pathlib import Path
    19	
    20	import numpy as np
    21	import torch
    22	from safetensors.numpy import save_file
    23	
    24	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    25	sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE / "dspark_train"))
    26	from drafter_body import build_body            # noqa
    27	GGUF = HERE.parent.parent / "ds4" / "gguf"
    28	DSPARK = str((GGUF / "dspark.gguf").resolve())
    29	TARGET = str((GGUF / "DeepSeek-V4-Flash-IQ2XXS-w2Q2K-AProjQ8-SExpQ8-OutQ8-chat-v2-imatrix.gguf").resolve())
    30	SHARDS = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data" / "shards"
    31	OUTDIR = HERE / "dspark_train" / "data"
    32	
    33	
    34	def main():
    35	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    36	    ap.add_argument("--splits", default="train,eval")
    37	    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0)
    38	    ap.add_argument("--offset", type=int, default=0, help="skip first N prompts of each split")
    39	    ap.add_argument("--count", type=int, default=0, help="process at most C prompts (0=all); saves a _part file")
    40	    args = ap.parse_args()
    41	    print("building torch body (MPS)...", flush=True)
    42	    body = build_body(DSPARK, TARGET, device="mps", dtype=torch.float32)
    43	    idx = json.loads((SHARDS / "index.json").read_text())
    44	    from safetensors import safe_open
    45	    for split in args.splits.split(","):
    46	        shards = sorted(SHARDS.glob(f"{split}_*.safetensors"))
    47	        mh_all, tok_all, pidx_all, pip_all = [], [], [], []
    48	        for f in shards:
    49	            with safe_open(f, "numpy") as d:
    50	                mh_all.append(d.get_tensor("main_hidden")); tok_all.append(d.get_tensor("token_ids"))
    51	                pidx_all.append(d.get_tensor("prompt_idx")); pip_all.append(d.get_tensor("pos_in_prompt"))
    52	        mh_all = np.concatenate(mh_all); tok_all = np.concatenate(tok_all)
    53	        pidx_all = np.concatenate(pidx_all); pip_all = np.concatenate(pip_all)
    54	        prompts_meta = [p for p in idx["prompts"] if p["split"] == split]
    55	        if args.limit:
    56	            prompts_meta = prompts_meta[: args.limit]
    57	        prompts_meta = prompts_meta[args.offset: args.offset + args.count] if args.count else prompts_meta[args.offset:]
    58	        part_tag = f"_part{args.offset}" if args.count else ""
    59	        xs, anc, tp1, pidxv = [], [], [], []
    60	        t0 = time.time(); n_done = 0
    61	        for pm in prompts_meta:
    62	            p_index = next(j for j, p in enumerate(idx["prompts"]) if p["prompt_id"] == pm["prompt_id"])
    63	            rows = np.where(pidx_all == p_index)[0]
    64	            rows = rows[np.argsort(pip_all[rows])]
    65	            mh = mh_all[rows]; toks = tok_all[rows]
    66	            G = len(toks)
    67	            if G < 4:
    68	                continue
    69	            n = G - 2
    70	            mh_seq = [np.asarray(mh[i], np.float32) for i in range(n + 1)]
    71	            anchors = [int(toks[s]) for s in range(1, n + 1)]
    72	            tgt = [int(toks[s + 1]) for s in range(1, n + 1)]
    73	            with torch.no_grad():
    74	                xv = body.forward_prompt(mh_seq, anchors).cpu().numpy().astype(np.float16)  # [n,BLOCK,HC,DIM]
    75	            xs.append(xv); anc.append(np.array(anchors, np.int64)); tp1.append(np.array(tgt, np.int64))
    76	            pidxv.append(np.full(n, p_index, np.int32))
    77	            n_done += 1
    78	            if n_done % 20 == 0:
    79	                el = time.time() - t0
    80	                print(f"  {split}: {n_done}/{len(prompts_meta)} prompts, {el:.0f}s ({el/n_done:.1f}s/prompt)", flush=True)
    81	        out = {
    82	            "x": np.concatenate(xs).astype(np.float16),
    83	            "anchor": np.concatenate(anc).astype(np.int64),
    84	            "target_p1": np.concatenate(tp1).astype(np.int64),
    85	            "prompt_idx": np.concatenate(pidxv).astype(np.int32),
    86	        }
    87	        fn = OUTDIR / f"{split}_torch_features{part_tag}.safetensors"
    88	        save_file({k: np.ascontiguousarray(v) for k, v in out.items()}, str(fn))
    89	        print(f"{split}: {out['x'].shape[0]} anchors -> {fn.name} ({time.time()-t0:.0f}s)", flush=True)
    90	
    91	
    92	if __name__ == "__main__":
    93	    main()

Building on this insight, the optimization can be evaluated along this greedy admission path. 

8 

We first globally sort all valid prefix extensions in descending order of survival probability. To dynamically determine the optimal target batch size _𝐵_ , we incrementally admit tokens from this sorted pool, updating the expected throughput Θ via an _𝑂_ (1) lookup from the pre-profiled cost table. 

Lossless speculative decoding strictly requires the _non-anticipating property_ : admission decisions must not depend on future candidate tokens (Chen et al., 2023; Leviathan et al., 2023). Because our confidence head relies on the Markov feature of the previously sampled token, computing the next survival probability _𝑎𝑟_ , _𝑘_ +1 explicitly requires the instantiated candidate _𝑥𝑟_ , _𝑘_ . A retrospective global search would thus inadvertently leak _𝑥𝑟_ , _𝑘_ into the admission decision for step _𝑘_ , introducing selection bias (we provide a concrete counterexample demonstrating this theoretical violation in Appendix A). 

To enforce strict causality, the scheduler (Algorithm 1) employs an early-stopping mechanism. By breaking the greedy search immediately when the throughput drops (Θ ≤ Θbest), the truncation decision relies solely on the prefix processed up to that exact step. This isolates the admission event from future tokens, ensuring exact target-distribution recovery. Note that this stepwise early-stopping yields the global maximum throughput if and only if the objective Θ is unimodal, which implicitly assumes a smoothly decaying hardware capacity curve. We address the engineering adaptations required for real-world, non-smooth SPS characteristics and asynchronous system pipelines in Section 5.2. 

## **3.3. Training** 

During training, we randomly sample multiple anchor positions from each target sequence to form _𝛾_ -token blocks as training data. The target model is frozen throughout training; the draft model shares its embedding layer and language modeling head and keeps them frozen, updating only the backbone drafter, sequential block, and confidence head. 

The training objective consists of three terms: a cross-entropy loss Lce, a distributionmatching loss Ltv, and a confidence loss Lconf. All three are position-weighted by _𝑤𝑘_ = exp(−( _𝑘_ −1)/ _𝛾_ ) (Chen et al., 2026), which emphasizes earlier block positions that contribute more to the expected acceptance length under prefix-based verification. The cross-entropy loss Lce trains the drafter to predict the correct next token:

## _**3.2.1. Confidence Head**_ 

Drawing inspiration from Huang et al. (2024); Wang et al. (2026), the confidence head outputs a scalar estimate _𝑐𝑘_ ∈(0, 1) for each draft position _𝑘_ . Crucially, _𝑐𝑘_ models the _conditional_ probability that the draft token at position _𝑘_ will survive target verification, given that all preceding tokens in the block have been accepted. The architecture features a lightweight linear projection followed by a sigmoid function: 

**==> picture [288 x 14] intentionally omitted <==**

where _ℎ𝑘_ is the hidden state of the backbone and _𝑊_ 1 [ _𝑥𝑘_ −1] is the Markov Embedding from the previous draft token. We supervise _𝑐𝑘_ using the analytical acceptance rate per-step _𝑐_[∗] _𝑘_[.][This rate] is determined by the total variation distance between the draft distribution _𝑝𝑘[𝑑]_[and the target] distribution _𝑝[𝑡] 𝑘_[:] 

**==> picture [276 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

**Post-hoc Calibration.** Unlike threshold-based verification heuristics (Huang et al., 2024; Li et al., 2024b; Zhang et al., 2026b), which only require confidence scores to correctly rank draft token qualities, our hardware-aware scheduling approach (detailed in Section 3.2.2) precisely requires the absolute magnitudes of the cumulative acceptance probabilities to compute the expected acceptance length _𝜏_ . Because neural confidence estimates are often overconfident (Guo et al., 2017; Ovadia et al., 2019), using the raw confidence scores directly would distort the throughput estimation, leading to suboptimal scheduling. 

To address this, we introduce **Sequential Temperature Scaling (STS)** . Because each _𝑐𝑖_ models a conditional probability, the chain rule dictates that the joint probability of a draft prefix being accepted factorizes into the cumulative product[�] _𝑖_ ⩽ _𝑘[𝑐][𝑖]_[.][Using a held-out validation set, STS] calibrates this joint probability consecutively from left to right. Specifically, at each position _𝑘_ ∈{1, _. . ._ , _𝛾_ }, we perform a simple 1D grid search to find the optimal temperature scalar that minimizes the Expected Calibration Error (ECE) (Naeini et al., 2015) of the cumulative product, keeping the already-calibrated scores of all preceding positions fixed. Crucially, temperature scaling is an order-preserving transformation: it rectifies the predicted probabilities to match empirical acceptance rates without disrupting the relative draft token rankings learned by the confidence head.

    11	p=1 by **+0.60 pp** (0.8118 → 0.8179; McNemar p=0.012, net +43/7137 anchors) — a
    12	statistically-real but **practically-tiny** effect, far below the +5 pp reaffirm gate
    13	and below +1 pp after the required rank 32/64/128 sweep. Body/input-side LoRA
    14	(Activity 8) was not run: its trigger condition (Activity 7 < Activity 6 ceiling) is

    16	**input-invariant at p=1** (input_effect 0.0, input_shift_rate 1.85%), so input-side
    17	adaptation is expected to be negligible. The decision: **close the non-expert
    18	fine-tuning direction.**
    19	
    20	## Evidence (Activities 4/6/7/9)
    21	
    22	- **Activity 4 (crossed hidden/label oracle):** on 54 prefix-aligned cells, the
    23	  drafter is **input-invariant** at p=1 (input_effect 0.0; the p=1 prediction is
    24	  identical on IQ2XXS vs Q4-tap hidden states 98% of the time); **label/argmax drift
    25	  dominates** (label_effect 1.85 pp, label_shift_rate 9.26%). ⇒ the lever is the
    26	  output/head ordering, NOT the input representation.
    27	- **Activity 6 (from-scratch head ceiling [GATE]):** no-LoRA frozen baseline p=1 =
    28	  **0.8118** (matches the ~0.81 reference; confirms the self-consistent torch
    29	  baseline). From-scratch head trained on the frozen pre-head features reaches only
    30	  **0.7479** held-out (below the pretrained head) — from-scratch under-generalizes
    31	  (21k Q2 anchors vs the pretrained head's FP-teacher training), as the plan-review
    32	  codex predicted. **Overfit-to-eval 0.9947** ⇒ the head impl is correct and `h`
    33	  carries the target info (target recoverable). Gate decision: proceed to 7 (not an
    34	  info deficit).
    35	- **Activity 7 (head-only LoRA, rank sweep 32/64/128):** **+0.67 / +0.62 / +0.63 pp** —
    36	  **flat across rank** (more capacity does not help → information-limited, not
    37	  capacity-limited). Overfit-to-eval (rank 32) only 0.8223 — rank-32 LoRA cannot even
    38	  fit the eval set. Best +0.67 pp ⇒ **NOT-JUSTIFIED** for the head path.
    39	- **Activity 9 (final eval + McNemar):** head-LoRA rank 32 **+0.60 pp** (0.8118 →
    40	  0.8179); McNemar discordants pre-only 119 / post-only 162 (p=0.012) — a real but tiny
    41	  net effect. Activity 8 skipped (7 > 6 ceiling; A4 input-negligible).
    42	
    43	## Interpretation
    44	
    45	- The Q2-target drafter deficit is **shallow** (Stage 0: target usually rank-2 in the
    46	  drafter's distribution) and **label/ordering-side** (Activity 4), but it is **not
    47	  recoverable by non-expert head LoRA**: the head LoRA is information-limited (rank
    48	  sweep flat; can't fit eval) and yields only ~+0.6 pp.
    49	- Consistent with Stage 1 (raising tap-layer precision did not help acceptance) and
    50	  Stage 0's caveat that the shallow-error *shape* is not proof of *recoverability*.
    51	- The from-scratch ceiling (0.7479 < pretrained 0.8118) signals the Q2 features
    52	  generalize worse than the FP features the pretrained head learned on — i.e. the Q2
    53	  mismatch does degrade the feature information, but not in a way a non-expert head
    54	  LoRA can exploit for a material gain.
    55	
    56	## Methodology notes (self-consistency + chaos)
    57	
    58	- The torch MPS drafter (body+head, float32) is used **self-consistently** for features,
    59	  baseline, and eval; the decision rule measures *relative* improvement, valid regardless
    60	  of the inherent float32 BLAS chaos offset vs the numpy oracle.
    61	- **Head bit-faithful** to numpy `forward_head` (argmax 100%, top-128 100%, base
    62	  max-abs 3e-5). **Body structurally faithful** (layer-0 parity max-abs ~2e-4 after the
    63	  codex-found Sinkhorn first-row eps-placement fix); deeper-layer float32 BLAS chaos is
    64	  inherent (a numpy replay reproduces it from BLAS differences alone) and accepted.
    65	- Codex review of this verdict + methodology: retained at
    66	  `artifacts/stage2_plan_review/` (this run) and `/tmp/` (body-bug review).
    67	
    68	## Recommendation
    69	
    70	- **Close the non-expert fine-tuning direction** (head LoRA: +0.6 pp; body LoRA not
    71	  run, gated out by Activity 4 + the 7>6 condition).
    72	- **Expert tuning** is the remaining untested lever but is **out of scope** (separate
    73	  workstream). If pursued later, the from-scratch ceiling + crossed-oracle findings
    74	  suggest it would need to address the Q2 feature degradation upstream of the head.
    75	- Otherwise, redirect to **server-side multi-request batching** (amortize the verify
    76	  bandwidth floor) — the local single-request speculative path on this target/drafter
    77	  pair has now exhausted drafter-precision (Stage 0 quantization ceiling), tap-precision
    78	  (Stage 1), tree structure (`spec_speedup_model.md` Q3), and non-expert fine-tuning

 succeeded in 0ms:
    68	## Loss design (DSpark §3.3)
    69	
    70	`L = Lce + Ltv` (the paper's `Lconf` is dropped — the confidence head serves the
    71	scheduler's admission, not argmax acceptance). **Exponential position weights**
    72	`w_k = exp(−(k−1)/γ)`, γ=5, emphasize p=1 — the decision metric and highest-leverage
    73	position.
    74	- **Lce**: cross-entropy to the target argmax (teacher-forced prev tokens). No label
    75	  smoothing.
    76	- **Ltv**: total-variation distance between draft and target distributions — *directly
    77	  maximizes expected acceptance* (paper). Computed over the captured **top-128
    78	  logprobs** (+ tail bucket); TV is the principled loss for this metric.

## Verdict soundness

V1 **likely-wrong**: current “head-LoRA” is effectively norm-only. Both LoRA factors are zero-init in [drafter_head.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/drafter_head.py:49), then used as `B @ A`; zero/zero gives zero gradients. Runnable check output: all `lora_*_A/B grad_max=0 delta_max=0`; only `lora_norm` moved. Rank flatness is therefore a training bug, not evidence of an information ceiling.

V2 **questionable**: Activity 6 is internally inconsistent. Saved gate says `"h lacks info -> consider 8"` in [activity6_summary.json](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/data/activity6_summary.json:5), while the verdict says proceed to 7 / not info deficit in [stage2_finetune_result.md](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_result.md:32). Overfit-to-eval proves fit/memorization capacity, not held-out recoverability.

V3 **likely-wrong**: skipping Activity 8 is premature. No body-LoRA runner exists; [drafter_body.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/drafter_body.py:83) is frozen and `build_body` has no LoRA args. A4 is only 54 aligned cells at frozen p=1 in [summary.json](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/artifacts/stage2_crossed_oracle/summary.json:11), not a proof body adaptation cannot help.

V4 **questionable**: self-consistent torch relative eval is basically valid, but scripts use unseeded `torch.randperm` in [run_stage2_head_lora.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/run_stage2_head_lora.py:51). Protocol required a data/seed sweep before NOT-JUSTIFIED in [stage2_finetune_protocol.md](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md:32).

V5 **sound numerically**: McNemar p recomputes to `0.0120915`, net `+43/7137`; effect is real but below gates. But JSON text saying “McNemar not significant” is wrong in [activity9_final.json](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/data/activity9_final.json:15).

V6 **questionable**: p=1 is preregistered primary, but the intended DSpark-style loss is K-weighted `Lce+Ltv` in [stage2_finetune_protocol.md](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md:70). Actual features/harness keep only `target_p1` and train `x[:,0]` CE.

## Highest-risk hole that could flip the verdict

Fix LoRA init, rerun head-LoRA. Standard init should be one factor random and the other zero, not both zero. Current +0.6 pp is not a head-LoRA ceiling; it is mostly `lora_norm` tuning.

## Concrete experiments to run

1. Correct LoRA init, rerun rank 32/64/128 p=1 CE, 3 seeds. Signal: if >1 pp, current NOT-JUSTIFIED fails; if >5 pp, REAFFIRM. Effort: low.
2. Add a param-delta assertion to the harness. Signal: all LoRA A/B factors must have nonzero delta after first epoch. Effort: trivial.
3. Run K=5 teacher-forced weighted CE + top-128 TV from shards. Signal: p=1 plus prefix hist / expected accepted tokens. Effort: medium.
4. Implement/run body LoRA despite the 7>6 proxy. Signal: tests the actual A6 “consider 8” path. Effort: medium/high.
5. Recheck from-scratch ceiling with seed/lr/epoch sweep. Signal: separates undertraining from real held-out ceiling. Effort: medium.

## Bottom line

Current NOT-JUSTIFIED does **not** survive adversarial review. The key head-LoRA experiment is invalidated by dead LoRA factors, and Activity 8 was skipped despite the saved A6 gate pointing toward it. The defensible verdict is **INCONCLUSIVE pending corrected LoRA + seed/loss sweep**.

Overfit-to-eval proves fit/memorization capacity, not held-out recoverability.

V3 **likely-wrong**: skipping Activity 8 is premature. No body-LoRA runner exists; [drafter_body.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/drafter_body.py:83) is frozen and `build_body` has no LoRA args. A4 is only 54 aligned cells at frozen p=1 in [summary.json](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/artifacts/stage2_crossed_oracle/summary.json:11), not a proof body adaptation cannot help.

V4 **questionable**: self-consistent torch relative eval is basically valid, but scripts use unseeded `torch.randperm` in [run_stage2_head_lora.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/run_stage2_head_lora.py:51). Protocol required a data/seed sweep before NOT-JUSTIFIED in [stage2_finetune_protocol.md](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md:32).

V5 **sound numerically**: McNemar p recomputes to `0.0120915`, net `+43/7137`; effect is real but below gates. But JSON text saying “McNemar not significant” is wrong in [activity9_final.json](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_train/data/activity9_final.json:15).

V6 **questionable**: p=1 is preregistered primary, but the intended DSpark-style loss is K-weighted `Lce+Ltv` in [stage2_finetune_protocol.md](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/summaries/stage2_finetune_protocol.md:70). Actual features/harness keep only `target_p1` and train `x[:,0]` CE.

## Highest-risk hole that could flip the verdict

Fix LoRA init, rerun head-LoRA. Standard init should be one factor random and the other zero, not both zero. Current +0.6 pp is not a head-LoRA ceiling; it is mostly `lora_norm` tuning.

## Concrete experiments to run

1. Correct LoRA init, rerun rank 32/64/128 p=1 CE, 3 seeds. Signal: if >1 pp, current NOT-JUSTIFIED fails; if >5 pp, REAFFIRM. Effort: low.
2. Add a param-delta assertion to the harness. Signal: all LoRA A/B factors must have nonzero delta after first epoch. Effort: trivial.
3. Run K=5 teacher-forced weighted CE + top-128 TV from shards. Signal: p=1 plus prefix hist / expected accepted tokens. Effort: medium.
4. Implement/run body LoRA despite the 7>6 proxy. Signal: tests the actual A6 “consider 8” path. Effort: medium/high.
5. Recheck from-scratch ceiling with seed/lr/epoch sweep. Signal: separates undertraining from real held-out ceiling. Effort: medium.

## Bottom line

Current NOT-JUSTIFIED does **not** survive adversarial review. The key head-LoRA experiment is invalidated by dead LoRA factors, and Activity 8 was skipped despite the saved A6 gate pointing toward it. The defensible verdict is **INCONCLUSIVE pending corrected LoRA + seed/loss sweep**.
